\vdash\mathbf{case}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\}
.

Similar to the previous case.

•

Rules
ST-SubLD
and
ST-SubRD
. Follows by IH.

∎

Theorem C.7 (Progress (Theorem
3.13
)).

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
ST-VarD
. Does not apply since the context here is empty.

•

Rule
ST-AppD
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{m}}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
where
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\ell}{}^{\mathit{m}}\!\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell\>\sqcup\>\mathit{m}}\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{m}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{b}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{b}
is a value, then
\mathit{b}=\lambda^{\mathit{m}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
for some
\mathit{b^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{m}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{m}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{m}}
.

•

Rule
ST-LetPairD
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{m}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
where
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}{}^{\mathit{m}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\times\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathit{x}:^{\ell\>\sqcup\>\mathit{m}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\ell}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{m}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{a}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{a}
is a value, then
\mathit{a}=(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{m}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
. Therefore,

\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{m}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{y}\}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{m}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{m}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

•

Rule
ST-LetUnitD
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathbf{let}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
where
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathbf{Unit}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathbf{let}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{a}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{a}
is a value, then
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{unit}
. Therefore,

\vdash\mathbf{let}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathbf{let}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathbf{let}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

•

Rule
ST-CaseD
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
where
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\ell}\mathit{B}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{a}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{a}
is a value, then
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
or
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Then,
\vdash\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\}
or
\vdash\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

•

Rules
ST-SubLD
and
ST-SubRD
. Follows by IH.

•

Rules
ST-LamD
,
ST-PairD
, and
ST-UnitD
. The terms typed by these rules are values.

∎

Appendix D
Heap Semantics For Simply-Typed LDC

Lemma D.1 (Similarity).

If
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
, then
\mathit{a}\{\mathit{H}\}=\mathit{a^{\prime}}\{\mathit{H^{\prime}}\}
or
\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{H}\}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}\{\mathit{H^{\prime}}\}
. Here,
\mathit{a}\{\mathit{H}\}
denotes the term obtained by substituting in
\mathit{a}
the definitions in
\mathit{H}
, in reverse order.

Proof.

By induction on
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.
∎

Lemma D.2 (Unchanged (Lemma
4.1
)).

If
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
(where
\lvert H_{1}\rvert=\lvert H^{\prime}_{1}\rvert
) and
\neg(\exists q_{0},r=q+q_{0})
, then
\mathit{r^{\prime}}=r
.

Proof.

By induction on
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
. There are two interesting cases:

•

Rule
HeapStep-Var
. Have:
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
, where
c
is a variable that is defined either in
\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
or in
\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. In either case,
\mathit{r^{\prime}}=r
. Observe that
\mathit{c}
cannot be
\mathit{x}
because then this rule would not apply, owing to the condition,
\neg(\exists q_{0},r=q+q_{0})
.

•

Rule
HeapStep-Discard
. We case split based on the parametrizing structure:

–

When the parametrizing structure is
\mathbb{N}_{=}
, this rule is superfluous.

–

When the parametrizing structure is
\mathbb{N}_{\geq}
, we have:

[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q^{\prime}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
where
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{q}<:\mathit{q^{\prime}}
.
Further,
\neg(\exists q_{0},r=q^{\prime}+q_{0})
.

Now, since
\mathit{q}<:\mathit{q^{\prime}}
, we know,
\exists\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q}=\mathit{q^{\prime}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
. Then, if for some
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, the equation
\mathit{r}=\mathit{q}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
holds, we would reach a contradiction because in that case, the equation,
\mathit{r}=\mathit{q^{\prime}}+(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
, would also hold.

Therefore,
\neg(\exists q_{0},r=q+q_{0})
.

This case, then, follows by IH.

–

When the parametrizing structure is a lattice
\mathcal{L}
, we have:

[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{m}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{m^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
where
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{m}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\ell}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{m^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
and
\ell\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime}
.
Further,
\neg(\mathit{m}\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime})
.

Then,
\neg(\mathit{m}\sqsubseteq\ell)
because otherwise,
\mathit{m}\sqsubseteq\ell\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime}
.

This case, then, follows by IH.

∎

Lemma D.3 (Irrelevant (Lemma
4.2
)).

If
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
(where
\lvert\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rvert=\lvert\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rvert
) and
\neg(\exists q_{0},r=q+q_{0})
, then
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{b},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{a}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{b},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
.
∎

Lemma D.4.

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that:

•

[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}

•

\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

•

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

(Note that
+
is overloaded here:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
denotes addition of contexts
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
after padding them as necessary.)

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
ST-Var
. Have
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A},0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}})+(0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A},0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

By inversion,
\exists\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q},\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
such that
H=\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q=\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
.

By lemma
B.3
,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{01}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{01}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{01}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}}
.

Then, we have,

–

[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{x}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{a}
.

–

\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}})+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}},\mathit{x}:^{0}\mathit{A},0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}},\mathit{x}:^{0}\mathit{A},0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
ST-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}{}^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

By IH, if
\mathit{b}
steps, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{a}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}{}^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

Otherwise,
\mathit{b}
is a value. By inversion
\mathit{b}=\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

Also, by inversion,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b^{\prime}})\>\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}
(assuming
\mathit{x}\>\textit{fresh}
).

Further,
\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}),\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
ST-LetUnit
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathbf{Unit}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

By IH, if
\mathit{a}
steps, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathbf{Unit}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

By rule
HeapStep-Discard
,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
. (
\because\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:1
)

And by rule
ST-LetUnit
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Otherwise,
\mathit{a}
is a value. By inversion,
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{unit}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H}]\mathit{b}
.

Further,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
. (
\because
In case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}
,
\overline{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}}=\overline{0}
and in case of
\mathbb{N}_{\geq}
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}<:\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}
.)

•

Rule
ST-LetPair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{}^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\times\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

By IH, if
\mathit{a}
steps, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{}^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\times\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

By rule
HeapStep-Discard
,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
. (
\because\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:1
)

And by rule
ST-LetPair
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:\>\mathit{B}
.

Otherwise,
\mathit{a}
is a value. By inversion,
\mathit{a}=(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.

Further,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{211}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{212}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{211}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{212}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{211}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{212}}}
.

Now,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}
(assuming
x,\mathit{y}\>\textit{fresh}
).

And,
\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}),\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

•

Rule
ST-Case
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

By IH, if
\mathit{a}
steps, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{}^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\times\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

By rule
HeapStep-Discard
,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. (
\because\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:1
)

And by rule
ST-Case
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Otherwise,
\mathit{a}
is a value. By inversion,
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
or
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Say
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
. Then,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Now,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
(assuming
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\textit{fresh}
).

And,
\mathit{H},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}),\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

The case when
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
is similar.

•

Rule
ST-SubL
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Since
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, there exists
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
. (In case of
\mathbb{N}_{=},\overline{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\overline{0}
.)

By IH, if
\mathit{a}
is not value, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}})+\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Then, by rule
ST-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}+\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, since
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}+\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

•

Rule
ST-SubR
. Have
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q^{\prime}}}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{q}<:\mathit{q^{\prime}}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

By IH, if
\mathit{a}
is not a value, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Then, since
\mathit{q}<:\mathit{q^{\prime}}
, by rule
HeapStep-Discard
,
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q^{\prime}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
. And by rule
ST-SubR
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q^{\prime}}}\mathit{A}
.

∎

Theorem D.5 (Soundness (Theorem
4.3
)).

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

Use lemma
D.4
with
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=0\cdot\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.
∎

Lemma D.6.

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcap\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
l\neq\top
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that:

•

[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\ell}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}

•

\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcap\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

•

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
. Follow lemma
D.4
.
∎

Theorem D.7 (Soundness (Theorem
4.3
)).

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
l\neq\top
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\ell}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

Use lemma
D.6
with
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=\top\sqcup\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.
∎

Corollary D.8 (No Usage (Corollary
4.4
)).

In LDC(
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbb{N}}
): Let
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{1}{}^{0}\!\mathit{A}\to\mathit{A}
. Then, for any
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{0}\mathit{A}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{0}\mathit{A}
, the terms
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}
and
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}
have the same operational behavior.

Proof.

To see why, we consider the reduction of
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}
and
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}
.

Let
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
denote a reduction of
\mathit{j}
steps where
\mathit{H}
and
\mathit{a}
are the initial heap and term,
\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{a^{\prime}}
are the final heap and term, and
\mathit{q}
is the label at which the reduction takes place.

For some
\mathit{j}
,
\mathit{H}
and
\mathit{b}
, we have,
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}
and
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b}
and
[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}
(for
\mathit{x}\>\textit{fresh}
).

But, if
[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}
, then
[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}
, for any
\mathit{k}
(By Lemma
4.2
).

Therefore,
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}
and
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}
have the same operational behavior.

∎

Corollary D.9 (Noninterference (Corollary
4.5
)).

In LDC(
\mathcal{L}
): Let
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{\mathbf{L}}{}^{\mathbf{H}}\!\mathit{A}\to\mathit{A}
. Then, for any
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathbf{H}}\mathit{A}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathbf{H}}\mathit{A}
, the terms
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathbf{H}}
and
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{\mathbf{H}}
have the same operational behavior.

Proof.

Same as the proof of Corollary
D.8
with
0
and
1
replaced by
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{L}
respectively.
∎

Corollary D.10 (Affine Usage (Corollary
4.6
)).

In LDC(
\mathbb{N}_{\geq}
): Let
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{1}{}^{1}\!\mathit{A}\to\mathit{A}
. Then, for any
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
, the term
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a}^{1}
uses
\mathit{a}
at most once during reduction.

Proof.

To see why, consider the reduction of
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a}^{1}
.

For some
\mathit{j}
,
\mathit{H}
and
\mathit{b}
, we have,
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{1}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a}^{1}
.

Then,
[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{1}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{1}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{b}
(for
\mathit{x}\>\textit{fresh}
).

Now,
\mathit{b}
may reduce to a value without ever looking-up for
\mathit{x}
or
\mathit{b}
may look-up the value of
\mathit{x}
exactly once. A single use of
\mathit{x}
will change the allowed usage from
1
to
0
, making it essentially unusable thereafter. Hence,
\mathit{a}
cannot be used more than once.
∎

Appendix E
Linearity Analysis in PTS Version of LDC

Lemma E.1 (Multiplication).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, then
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. Follow the proof of lemma
B.1
.
∎

Lemma E.2 (Factorization).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then there exists
\Gamma^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. Follow the proof of lemma
B.2
.
∎

Lemma E.3 (Splitting).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
, then there exists
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

If
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=0
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=0\cdot\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.

Otherwise, by Lemma
E.2
,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma<:(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Then,
\Gamma=(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
for some
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
(in case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}
,
\overline{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\overline{0}
).

Now, by Lemma
E.1
,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

By rule
PTS-SubL
,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

The lemma follows by setting
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
∎

Lemma E.4 (Weakening).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{C}:\mathit{s}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{0}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.
∎

Lemma E.5 (Substitution (Lemma
5.1
)).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
PTS-Var
. Have:
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
where
\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{A}:\mathit{s}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\Delta
. There are two cases to consider.

–

Have:
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
where
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. Follows from what’s given.

–

Have:
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{0}\mathit{C},0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{0}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma,0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Follows by rules
PTS-Var
and
PTS-SubL
(note that in case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}
,
\overline{\Gamma}=\overline{0}
).

•

Rule
PTS-Weak
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:^{0}\mathit{B}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\Delta
. There are two cases to consider.

–

Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:^{0}\mathit{B}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B}
where
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{b}/\mathit{y}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{b}/\mathit{y}\}
.

Since
y\notin\>\text{fv }a
and
y\notin\>\text{fv }A
, need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

This case follows by rule
PTS-SubL
(note that in case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}
,
\overline{\Gamma}=\overline{0}
).

–

Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}},\mathit{y}:^{0}\mathit{B}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{y}:^{0}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Follows by IH and rule
PTS-Weak
.

•

Rule
PTS-Pi
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{3}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{A}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{B}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathcal{R}(\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{3}}}})
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{3}}}}
.

By lemma
E.3
,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}=\Gamma
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Pi
.

•

Rule
PTS-Lam
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
where

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Lam
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\>\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\{\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}/\mathit{x}\}
.

By lemma
E.3
,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}=\Gamma
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}

and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-App
.

•

Rule
PTS-Conv
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{A}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Also, since
\mathit{A}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}
, so
\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Conv
.

•

Rule
PTS-Pair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):^{\mathit{q}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}):^{\mathit{q}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

By lemma
E.3
,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}

and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Now,
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}=\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}/\mathit{x}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Pair
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetPair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/w\}
where

\Delta,w:\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor,\mathit{z}:\mathit{C},\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\rfloor
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/w\}
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\{\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}/w\}
.

By lemma
E.3
,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/w\}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-LetPair
.

•

Rules
PTS-Sum
,
PTS-Inj1
, and
PTS-Inj2
. By IH.

•

Rule
PTS-Case
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/w\}
where

\Delta,w:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor,\mathit{z}:\mathit{C},\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\rfloor
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/w\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/w\}
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\rfloor
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\{\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}/w\}
.

By lemma
E.3
,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{31}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/w\}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
and

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{32}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/w\}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Case
.

•

Rules
PTS-SubL
and
PTS-SubR
. By IH.

∎

Theorem E.6 (Preservation (Theorem
5.2
)).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and inversion on
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Let
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
. By inversion:

–

\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}
, when
\vdash\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Follows by IH and rule
PTS-App
.

–

\mathit{b}=\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
and
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

By inversion on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
, we get
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{B}
for some
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}
and
\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}=_{\beta}\mathit{A}
.

Now, there are two cases to consider.

*

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=0
. Since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}
, so
\mathit{q}=0
.

Then, by rule
PTS-Conv
and the substitution lemma,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}:^{0}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

*

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
. By lemma
E.2
,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathit{r^{\prime}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{1}\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}}
.

Since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
, therefore
\mathit{r}<:\mathit{r^{\prime}}
. Hence, by rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{1}\mathit{B}
.

Now, by lemma
E.1
,
\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
. By rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Conv
and the substitution lemma.

•

Rule
PTS-LetUnit
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathbf{Unit}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathbf{Unit}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{unit}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Let
\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{c}
. By inversion:

–

\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
, when
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Follows by IH and rules
PTS-LetUnit
and
PTS-Conv
.

–

\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{unit}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Follows by rule
PTS-SubL
(note that in case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}
,
\overline{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\overline{0}
).

•

Rule
PTS-LetPair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta,\mathit{z}:\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
.

Let
\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{c}
. By inversion:

–

\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
, when
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Follows by IH and rules
PTS-LetPair
and
PTS-Conv
.

–

\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{y}\}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{y}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})/\mathit{z}\}
.

By inversion on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, we have:

\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}
.

Applying the substitution lemma, we get,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
.

Applying the lemma again, we get,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{y}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})/\mathit{z}\}
, as required.

•

Rule
PTS-Case
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Let
\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
. By inversion:

–

\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, when
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Follows by IH and rules
PTS-Case
and
PTS-Conv
.

–

\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\}
.

Need to show
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
.

By inversion on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, we have
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

This case, then, follows by applying the substitution lemma.

–

\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\}
.

Similar to the previous case.

•

Rules
PTS-Weak
,
PTS-Conv
,
PTS-SubL
, and
PTS-SubR
. Follows by IH.

∎

Theorem E.7 (Progress (Theorem
5.3
)).

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{b}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{b}
is a value, then, by inversion,
\mathit{b}=\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
for some
\mathit{b^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetUnit
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\mathit{z}:\mathbf{Unit}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathbf{Unit}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{unit}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Ned to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{a}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{a}
is a value, then, by inversion,
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{unit}
. Therefore,
\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathbf{unit}\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetPair
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\mathit{z}:\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{a}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{a}
is a value, then, by inversion,
\mathit{a}=(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
. Therefore,

\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{y}\}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

•

Rule
ST-Case
. Have:
\emptyset\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\mathit{z}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
and
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Need to show:
\exists c,\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
.

By IH,
\mathit{a}
is either a value or
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

If
\mathit{a}
is a value, then
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
or
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Then,
\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\}
or
\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\}
.

Otherwise,
\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

•

Rules
PTS-Weak
,
PTS-Conv
,
PTS-SubL
, and
PTS-SubR
. Follows by IH.

•

Rule
PTS-Var
. Does not apply since the context here is empty.

•

The terms typed by the remaining rules are values.

∎

Appendix F
Heap Semantics for PTS Version of LDC

Recall that to show heap soundness for a dependent type system, we need to allow delayed substitution in types. Towards this end, we extend contexts with definitions, which mimic substitutions. The definitions are only used to derive type equalities and are orthogonal to linearity and dependency analyses. The typing rules for definitions and the conversion rule where definitions are used are shown in Figure
13
. We add these extra rules to the type system of the calculus.

context
,
\Gamma::=\emptyset\>|\>\Gamma,\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\>|\>\Gamma,\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

(Extra Rules)

\frac{\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}\quad\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\Delta}{0\cdot\Gamma,\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:PTS-DefVar}{{{PTS-DefVar}}}
}{},}\mpr@andcr\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\quad\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{b}:\mathit{B}\quad\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\Delta}{\Gamma,\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:PTS-DefWeak}{{{PTS-DefWeak}}}
}{},}\mpr@andcr\frac{\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\quad\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}\quad\mathit{A}\{\Delta\}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}\{\Delta\}\quad\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\Delta}{\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:PTS-DefConv}{{{PTS-DefConv}}}
}{},}

Figure 13
.
Typing Rules With Definitions

There are a few things to note:

•

Definitions do not interact with the analyses, as we see in rules
PTS-DefVar
and
PTS-DefWeak
.

•

The conversion rule
PTS-DefConv
checks for equality of types after substituting the definitions in the types. Here,
\mathit{A}\{\Delta\}
denotes the type
\mathit{A}
with the definitions in
\Delta
substituted in reverse order. With this rule, we can show:
\mathit{x}=\mathbf{Unit}:^{0}\mathit{s}\vdash\lambda^{1}\mathit{y}:\mathit{x}.\mathit{y}:^{1}\Pi\mathit{y}:^{1}\!\mathbf{Unit}.\mathbf{Unit}
.

The definitions allow us to communicate to the type system the substitutions that have been delayed by the heap. To enable this communication, we also need to update the compatibility relation, as shown in Figure
14
.

\mathit{H}\models\Gamma

(Updated Compatibility)

\frac{}{\emptyset\models\emptyset}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:HeapCompat-DefEmpty}{{{HeapCompat-DefEmpty}}}
}{},}\mpr@andcr\frac{\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\quad\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}}{\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={\hypertarget{ottalt:rule:ott:HeapCompat-DefCons}{{{HeapCompat-DefCons}}}
}{},}

Figure 14
.
Compatibility Relation With Definitions

These extensions do not alter the essential character of the underlying system. The following lemmas establish the correspondence between the underlying system and the extended system. To distinguish, let us denote the typing and the compatibility judgments of the underlying system as
\Gamma\vdash^{\mathbf{u}}\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{u}}\Gamma
and those of the extended system as
\Gamma\vdash^{\mathbf{e}}\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{e}}\Gamma
respectively. Also, for
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{u}}\Gamma
, let
\Gamma_{\mathit{H}}
be the context
\Gamma
with the variables defined according to
\mathit{H}
. Then, the multi-substitution lemma below says that given a derivation in the extended system, substituting the definitions gives us a derivation in the underlying system. The elaboration lemma says that given a derivation in the underlying system, adding appropriate definitions to the context gives us a derivation in the extended system.

Lemma F.1 (Multi-Substitution).

If
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{e}}\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash^{\mathbf{e}}\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, then
\emptyset\vdash^{\mathbf{u}}\mathit{a}\{\Delta\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\Delta\}
where
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{e}}\Gamma
.
∎

Lemma F.2 (Elaboration).

If
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{u}}\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash^{\mathbf{u}}\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, then
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{e}}\Gamma_{\mathit{H}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathit{H}}\vdash^{\mathbf{e}}\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\mathit{H}\models^{\mathbf{u}}\Gamma
.
∎

Next, we prove the soundness theorem for the extended system.

Lemma F.3.

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that:

•

[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}

•

\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

•

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

(Note that
+
is overloaded here:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
denotes addition of contexts
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
after padding them as necessary.)

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
PTS-DefVar
. Have:
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
where
\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A})+(0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A})
.

By inversion,
\exists\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that
H=\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

By lemma
E.3
,
