\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{01}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{01}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{01}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}}
.

Then, we have,

–

[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{x}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{a}
.

–

\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A})+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{0}\mathit{A})
.

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{02}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{0}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
PTS-DefWeak
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{b}:\mathit{B}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{B})+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B})
.

By inversion,
\exists\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that
H=\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{b}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.

By IH,
\exists\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that

–

[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\{\mathit{y}\}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
where
\lvert\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rvert=\lvert\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rvert

–

\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{22}}}

–

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

Then, we have,

–

[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{b}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{b},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}

–

\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{b},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\models((\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{B})+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B})),\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{22}}}

–

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

•

Rules
PTS-Var
and
PTS-Weak
. Does not apply since whenever
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
holds, every assumption in
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is a definition.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

If
\mathit{b}
steps, then this case follows by IH.

Otherwise,
\mathit{b}
is a value. By inversion,
b=\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

Further,
\exists\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=_{\beta}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
where
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Then, by rule
PTS-DefConv
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
. Further,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Now,

–

[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}})\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}

–

\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}),\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
by rule
PTS-DefConv
.

•

Rule
PTS-DefConv
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{A}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rfloor
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

By IH,
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Let
\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rfloor
. Then,
\mathit{A}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=\mathit{A}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=\mathit{B}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
(
\because\textit{fv}\>\mathit{A},\textit{fv}\>\mathit{B}\in\text{dom}\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\subseteq\text{dom}\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
).

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-DefConv
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetPair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{z}:\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

If
\mathit{a}
steps, then
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}\,\cup\,\textit{fv}\>\mathit{b}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Then,

–

[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
.

By rule
HeapStep-Discard
,
[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}
. (
\because\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:1
)

–

\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})

–

By rule
ST-LetPair
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:\>\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a^{\prime}}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Now, by lemma
D.1
,
\mathit{a^{\prime}}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=_{\beta}\mathit{a}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
where
\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Then,
\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=\mathit{B}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\{\mathit{a}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}/\mathit{z}\}=\mathit{B}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\{\mathit{a}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}/\mathit{z}\}=_{\beta}\mathit{B}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}\{\mathit{a^{\prime}}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}/\mathit{z}\}=\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a^{\prime}}/\mathit{z}\}\{\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
.

By rule
PTS-DefConv
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:\>\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Otherwise,
\mathit{a}
is a value. By inversion,
a=(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.

Further,
\exists\Gamma_{{\mathrm{211}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{212}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{211}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{212}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{211}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{212}}}
.

Then,

–

[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}
(assuming
x,\mathit{y}\>\textit{fresh}
)

–

\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}),\mathit{x}=\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}=\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})/\mathit{z}\}
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetUnit
. Similar to rule
PTS-LetPair
.

•

Rule
PTS-Case
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{z}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{z}\}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

If
\mathit{a}
steps, then this case follows by IH.

Otherwise,
\mathit{a}
is a value. By inversion,
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
or
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Say
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
. Then,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Now,

–

[\mathit{H}]\mathbf{case}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
(assuming
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\textit{fresh}
)

–

\mathit{H},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}),\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{z}\}

The case when
\mathit{a}=\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
is similar.

∎

Theorem F.4 (Soundness (Theorem
5.4
)).

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

Use lemma
F.3
with
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=0\cdot\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.
∎

Corollary F.5 (Corollary
5.5
).

In LDC(
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbb{N}}
): If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{1}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{0}\!\mathit{s}.\mathit{x}
and
\emptyset\vdash_{0}\mathit{A}:\mathit{s}
, then
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}
must diverge.

Proof.

To see why, let us assume, towards contradiction, that
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}
terminates.

Let
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:=\mathbf{Unit}
and
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:=\mathbf{Bool}
.

Then, for some
\mathit{j}
,
\mathit{H}
and
\mathit{b}
, we have,
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}
and
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}
.

Again,
[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b}
and
[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}
(say,
\mathit{x}\>\textit{fresh}
).

Now, if
[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}
, then
[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}
, for any
\mathit{k}
(By Lemma
D.3
).

As and when the reduction stops,
\mathit{b^{\prime}}
is a value. By soundness,
\mathit{b^{\prime}}
must be a value of types
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
simultaneously, a contradiction. (
\because
there is no value that has types both
\mathbf{Unit}
and
\mathbf{Bool}
.)

So
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}
,
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}
, and therefore
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}
, must diverge for any
\mathit{A}
.

As a corollary, we see that in a strongly normalizing PTS, no such
\mathit{f}
exists.

Using the same argument as above, we can also show that if
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{\mathbf{L}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathbf{H}}\!\mathit{s}.\mathit{x}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{A}:^{\mathbf{H}}\mathit{s}
, then
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{\mathbf{H}}
must diverge.
∎

Corollary F.6 (Corollary
5.6
).

In LDC(
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbb{N}}
): In a strongly normalizing PTS, if
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{1}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{0}\!\mathit{s}.\Pi\mathit{y}:^{1}\!\mathit{x}.\mathit{x}
and
\emptyset\vdash_{0}\mathit{A}:\mathit{s}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
, then
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}\>\mathit{a}^{1}=_{\beta}\mathit{a}
.

Proof.

To see why, let’s look at how
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}\>\mathit{a}^{1}
reduces.

Let,
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:=\mathbf{Unit}
and
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:=\mathbf{Bool}
. Also, let
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:=\mathbf{unit}
and
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:=\mathbf{true}
.

Then, for some
\mathit{j}
,
\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{b}
, we have,
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}
and
[\emptyset]\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{j}}[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}
.

Now,
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}
and
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}}](\lambda^{0}\mathit{x}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}
(say
\mathit{x}\>\textit{fresh}
).

Then,
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}](\lambda^{1}\mathit{y}.\mathit{c})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}
and
[\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}](\lambda^{1}\mathit{y}.\mathit{c})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}
, for some
k
(By Lemma
D.3
).

Next,
[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}](\lambda^{1}\mathit{y}.\mathit{c})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{1}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{c}
and

[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}}](\lambda^{1}\mathit{y}.\mathit{c})\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{1}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{c}
(say
\mathit{y}\>\textit{fresh}
).

Since the reductions terminate, the value of
\mathit{y}
gets looked up. Till that look-up, the two reductions are indistinguishable from one another.

Let
[\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{1}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{c}\Longrightarrow^{1}_{\mathit{k^{\prime}}}[\mathit{H^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}\overset{0}{\mapsto}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{y}\overset{1}{\mapsto}\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{H_{{\mathrm{3}}}}]\mathit{c^{\prime}}
be the point at which
y
is looked-up. Analyzing the stepping rules,
\mathit{c^{\prime}}=y
or
\mathit{c^{\prime}}
is a proper path headed by
y
(where a path is a series of nested elimination forms headed by a variable). By soundness,
\mathit{c^{\prime}}
is well-typed. But there is no well-typed proper path that can be headed by both
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
because
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\>\mathbf{Unit}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\>\mathbf{Bool}
. Therefore,
\mathit{c^{\prime}}=y
.

This means that
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{1}
reduces to
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{0}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}^{1}
to
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, and hence
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}\>\mathit{a}^{1}
to
\mathit{a}
, for any
\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{a}
. Therefore,
\mathit{f}\>\mathit{A}^{0}\>\mathit{a}^{1}=_{\beta}\mathit{a}
.

∎

Appendix G
Dependency Analysis in PTS Version of LDC

Lemma G.1 (Multiplication).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
, then
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\sqcup\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
. Follow the proof of lemma
C.1
.
∎

Lemma G.2 (Splitting).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcap\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
, then there exists
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcap\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcap\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
. By rule
PTS-SubRD
,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
. The lemma follows by setting
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.
∎

Lemma G.3 (Weakening).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{C}:\mathit{s}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\top}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
.
∎

Lemma G.4 (Substitution (Lemma
5.1
)).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{m_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{m_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcap\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{m_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
. Follow lemma
C.4
.
∎

Theorem G.5 (Preservation (Theorem
5.2
)).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and inversion on
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
. Follow theorem
E.6
.

∎

Theorem G.6 (Progress (Theorem
5.3
)).

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
. Follow theorem
E.7
.

∎

Lemma G.7.

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcap\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
l\neq\top
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that:

•

[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\ell}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}

•

\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqcap\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

•

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
. Follow lemma
F.3
.
∎

Theorem G.8 (Soundness (Theorem
5.4
)).

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
and
l\neq\top
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\ell}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

Use lemma
G.7
with
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=\top\sqcup\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.
∎

Appendix H
Unrestricted Usage

Lemma H.1 (Multiplication).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, then
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. All the cases other than rule
PTS-LamOmega
are similar to those of lemma
E.1
.

•

Rule
PTS-LamOmega
. Have:
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma,\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{q}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
.

Need to show:
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
.

There are two cases to consider:

–

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=0
. By IH,
0\cdot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:^{0}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathit{B}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-LamOmega
.

–

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
. Therefore,
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-LamOmega
.

∎

Lemma H.2 (Independence).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{0}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. Here,
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}\triangleq\exists\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{q}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
PTS-Var
. Have
0\cdot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
where
\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{A}:\mathit{s}
and
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

This case follows immediately because
\mathit{q}\ll:\mathit{q}
and the other grades are all
0
.

•

Rule
PTS-Pi
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{3}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{A}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{B}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathcal{R}(\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{3}}}})
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

Now, if
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
or
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, then
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, a contradiction.

Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-Pi
.

•

Rule
PTS-LamOmega
. Have:
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{q}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
and
\neg(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\ll:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q})
.

If
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, then
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\ll:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}
, a contradiction. Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-LamOmega
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

There are two cases to consider.

–

\mathit{r}=0
. Then,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{0}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{0}\mathit{A}
.

Now, if
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, then
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, a contradiction. Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-App
.

–

\mathit{r}\neq 0
. Then,
\mathit{r}=1+\mathit{r^{\prime}}
, for some
\mathit{r^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-App
.

•

Rule
PTS-Pair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):^{\mathit{q}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

Here too, there are two cases to consider.

–

\mathit{r}=0
. Then,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{0}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{0}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Now, if
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, then
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}
, a contradiction. Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-Pair
.

–

\mathit{r}\neq 0
. Then,
\mathit{r}=1+\mathit{r^{\prime}}
, for some
\mathit{r^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r})
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-Pair
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetPair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta,\mathit{z}:\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{11}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{12}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:1
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

Since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:1
, so
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=1+\mathit{q^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
, for some
\mathit{q^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
. Therefore,
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{01}}}}\ll:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}})
and
\neg(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{02}}}}\ll:\mathit{q})
.

This case, then, follows by IH and rule
PTS-LetPair
.

•

The other cases follow similarly.

∎

Lemma H.3 (Factorization).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then there exists
\Gamma^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
PTS-Pi
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{3}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{A}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{B}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

By IH,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{A}:^{1}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{B}:^{1}\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Pi
.

•

Rule
PTS-LamOmega
. Have:
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma,\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{q}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
.

There are two cases to consider:

–

\mathit{q}=\omega
. Then
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}=\omega
.

Here, we need to define an operation on contexts. For a context
\Gamma
, define
\Gamma^{\{0,\omega\}}
as:

\displaystyle\emptyset^{\{0,\omega\}}

\displaystyle=\emptyset

\displaystyle(\Gamma,\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A})^{\{0,\omega\}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\Gamma^{\{0,\omega\}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\text{ if }q\in\{0,\omega\}\\
\Gamma^{\{0,\omega\}},\mathit{x}:^{0}\mathit{A}\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Now, since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\omega}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
, by lemma
H.2
,
(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma)^{\{0,\omega\}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\omega}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
.

By rule
PTS-SubR
,
(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma)^{\{0,\omega\}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{1}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
.

Next,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma<:(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma)^{\{0,\omega\}}=\omega\cdot(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma)^{\{0,\omega\}}=\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}\cdot(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma)^{\{0,\omega\}}
.

Note that in case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}^{\omega}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\text{Lin}}
, we have,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma=(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma)^{\{0,\omega\}}
.

–

\mathit{q}\neq\omega
. By IH,
\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{1}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}}
.

Since
\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}}
and
q\notin\{0,\omega\}
, so
\mathit{r}<:\mathit{r^{\prime}}
.

By rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{1}\mathit{B}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-LamOmega
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

There are two cases to consider:

–

\mathit{r}=0
. By IH,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{1}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

Next, by the multiplication lemma,
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{0}\mathit{A}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-App
.

–

\mathit{r}\neq 0
. By IH,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{1}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Then, by the multiplication lemma,
\mathit{r}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-App
.

•

Rule
PTS-Pair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):^{\mathit{q}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
.

There are two cases to consider.

–

r=0
. Then, by the multiplication lemma,
0\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{0}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

By IH,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{1}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Pair
.

–

r\neq 0
. By IH,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{1}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{1}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}\}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:(\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r})\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Then, by the multiplication lemma,
\mathit{r}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Pair
.

•

Rule
PTS-LetPair
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Delta,\mathit{z}:\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{B}:\mathit{s}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
.

There are two cases to consider:

–

\mathit{q}=\omega
. Now, by lemma
H.2
,
(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})^{\{0,\omega\}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{\omega}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
.

By rule
PTS-SubR
,
(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})^{\{0,\omega\}}\vdash\mathbf{let}_{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\>(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{b}:^{1}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Next,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})^{\{0,\omega\}}=\omega\cdot(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})^{\{0,\omega\}}
.

Note that in case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}^{\omega}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\text{Lin}}
, we have,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}=(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})^{\{0,\omega\}}
.

–

\mathit{q}\neq\omega
. By IH,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{1}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Now, since
q\notin\{0,\omega\}
, so
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Therefore, by rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{1}\mathit{B}\{(\mathit{x}^{\mathit{r}},\mathit{y})/\mathit{z}\}
.

Next, by the multiplication lemma,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\Sigma\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-LetPair
.

•

The other cases follow similarly.

∎

Lemma H.4 (Splitting).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
, then there exists
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof.

If
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=0
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=0\cdot\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.

Otherwise, by lemma
H.3
,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma<:(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Then,
\Gamma=(\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
for some
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
. In case of
\mathbb{N}_{=}^{\omega}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\text{Lin}}
, we choose
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}^{\{0,\omega\}}
.

Now, by lemma
H.1
,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

Next,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
. Then, by rule
PTS-SubL
,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\mathit{A}
.

The lemma follows by setting
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.
∎

Lemma H.5 (Weakening).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{0}\mathit{C}:\mathit{s}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{0}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.
∎

Lemma H.6 (Substitution).

If
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
and
\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
, then
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{a}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. All the cases other than rule
PTS-LamOmega
are similar to those of lemma
E.5
.

•

Rule
PTS-LamOmega
. Have:
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}
and
\mathit{q}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
.

Further,
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
where
\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rfloor
.

There are two cases to consider:

–

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=0
. Then,
0\cdot\Gamma\vdash\mathit{c}:^{0}\mathit{C}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rules
PTS-LamOmega
and
PTS-SubL
.

–

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
. Then
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq 0
.

By the factorization lemma,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{1}\mathit{C}
where
\Gamma<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}
.

By the multiplication lemma,
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{c}:^{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{C}
.

By IH,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

By rule
PTS-LamOmega
,
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{b}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}.\mathit{B}\{\mathit{c}/\mathit{z}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-SubL
.

∎

Lemma H.7 (Lambda Inversion).

If
\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{C}
, then
\exists\mathit{A^{\prime}},\mathit{B^{\prime}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
such that:

•

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}

•

\Gamma<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}

•

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\neq 0

•

\mathit{A}=_{\beta}\mathit{A^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{C}=_{\beta}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{B^{\prime}}
and
\Delta\vdash_{0}\mathit{C}:\mathit{s}
, where
\Delta=\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{C}
.
∎

Theorem H.8 (Preservation).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and inversion on
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
. All the cases other than rule
PTS-App
are similar to those of lemma
E.6
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Let
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{c}
. By inversion:

–

\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}
, when
\vdash\mathit{b}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Follows by IH and rule
PTS-App
.

–

\mathit{b}=\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
and
\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\leadsto\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Applying lemma
H.7
on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
, we get:

*

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}

*

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}

*

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\neq 0

*

\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}=_{\beta}\mathit{A^{\prime}}
and
\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}=_{\beta}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}.\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Now, there are three cases to consider.

*

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=0
. Since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}
, so
\mathit{q}=0
.

Then, by the substitution lemma and rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}:^{0}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

*

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\omega
. So
\mathit{r}=\omega
.

Now, if
\mathit{q}=0
, then by the substitution lemma and rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}:^{0}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

If
\mathit{q}\neq 0
, then
\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}=\omega
. So
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\omega}\mathit{A}
.

Next,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{x}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\omega}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

By the multiplication lemma and rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\omega}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

By the substitution lemma,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}:^{\omega}\mathit{B^{\prime}}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

This case, then, follows by rules
PTS-Conv
and
PTS-SubR
.

*

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\notin\{0,\omega\}
. By lemma
H.3
,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\mathit{r^{\prime}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{1}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}}
.

Since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\notin\{0,\omega\}
, so
\mathit{r}<:\mathit{r^{\prime}}
. Hence, by rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{1}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Now, by lemma
H.1
,
\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
. By rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
PTS-Conv
and the substitution lemma.

∎

Theorem H.9 (Progress).

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. Follow the proof of theorem
E.7
.
∎

Note:
The multi-substitution and elaboration lemmas (Lemmas
F.1
and
F.2
) are true of LDC(
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbb{N}}^{\omega}
).

Lemma H.10.

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that:

•

[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}

•

\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

•

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
. All the cases other than rule
PTS-App
are similar to those of lemma
F.3
.

•

Rule
PTS-App
. Have:
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\mathit{b}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A}
.

Further,
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}})
.

If
\mathit{b}
steps, then this case follows by IH.

Otherwise,
\mathit{b}
is a value. By inversion,
b=\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}
.

Using lemma
H.7
on
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\vdash\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}
, we get:

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{20}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}

–

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathrm{20}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\cdot\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}<:\mathit{q}

–

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\omega\Rightarrow\mathit{r}=\omega
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\neq 0

–

\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=_{\beta}\mathit{A^{\prime}}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
and
\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A}.\mathit{B}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}=_{\beta}\Pi\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\!\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}.\mathit{B^{\prime}}\{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}
where
\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\lfloor\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}\rfloor
.

Now, there are two cases to consider.

–

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\omega
. So
\mathit{r}=\omega
. Further, since
\mathit{q}\neq 0
, so
\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}=\omega
.

So,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{20}}},\mathit{x}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\omega}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

By the multiplication lemma and rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\omega}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Then, by rule
PTS-DefConv
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}
.

Further,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\omega}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
. And by rule
PTS-SubR
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Now,

*

[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}})\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\omega}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}

*

\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\omega}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}),\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}

*

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\omega}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
by rule
PTS-DefConv
.

–

\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\neq\omega
. Note that if
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=0
, then
\mathit{q}=0
, a contradiction. So
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\notin\{0,\omega\}
.

By lemma
H.3
,
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{20}}}
and
\mathit{r^{\prime}}
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{20}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r^{\prime}}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{1}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{20}}}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{20}}}
and
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r}<:\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\cdot\mathit{r^{\prime}}
.

Since
\mathit{q_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\notin\{0,\omega\}
, therefore
\mathit{r}<:\mathit{r^{\prime}}
. Hence, by rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{20}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{1}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Now, by lemma
H.1
,
\mathit{q}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{20}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
. By rule
PTS-SubL
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Next, by rule
PTS-DefConv
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{22}}}\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}
. Then,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B^{\prime}}
.

Therefore,

*

[\mathit{H}](\lambda^{\mathit{r}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}.\mathit{b^{\prime}})\>\mathit{a}^{\mathit{r}}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}]\mathit{b^{\prime}}

*

\mathit{H},\mathit{x}\overset{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}{\mapsto}\mathit{a}\models(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}}),\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}

*

\Gamma_{{\mathrm{21}}},\mathit{x}=\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}\cdot\mathit{r}}\mathit{A^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\mathit{b^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{B}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
by rule
PTS-DefConv
.

∎

Theorem H.11 (Soundness).

If
\mathit{H}\models\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
and
q\neq 0
, then either
\mathit{a}
is a value or there exists
\mathit{H^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{a^{\prime}}
such that
[\mathit{H}]\mathit{a}\Longrightarrow^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathit{S}}[\mathit{H^{\prime}}]\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\models\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathit{a^{\prime}}:^{\mathit{q}}\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

Use lemma
H.10
with
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}:=0\cdot\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}:=\Gamma
.
∎

Theorem H.12 (Theorem
6.1
).

LDC(
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbb{N}}^{\omega}
) satisfies type soundness and heap soundness.

Proof.

Follows by theorems
H.8
,
H.9
and
H.11
.
∎

Appendix I
Comparison of LDC with Other Calculi

Theorem I.1 (Theorem
7.1
).

The translation from LNL
\lambda
-calculus to LDC(
\mathcal{Q}_{\text{Lin}}
), shown in Figure
10
, is sound:

•

If
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash_{\mathcal{L}}\mathit{e}:\mathit{A}
, then
\overline{\Theta}^{\omega},\overline{\Gamma}^{1}\vdash\overline{\mathit{e}}:^{1}\overline{\mathit{A}}
.

•

If
\Theta\vdash_{\mathcal{C}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{X}
, then
\overline{\Theta}^{\omega}\vdash\overline{\mathit{t}}:^{\omega}\overline{\mathit{X}}
.

•

If
\mathit{e}=_{\beta}\mathit{f}
, then
\overline{\mathit{e}}=_{\beta}\overline{\mathit{f}}
. If
\mathit{s}=_{\beta}\mathit{t}
then
\overline{\mathit{s}}=_{\beta}\overline{\mathit{t}}
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash_{\mathcal{L}}\mathit{e}:\mathit{A}
and
\Theta\vdash_{\mathcal{C}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{X}
for typing.

By case analysis on
\mathit{e}=_{\beta}\mathit{f}
and
\mathit{s}=_{\beta}\mathit{t}
for
\beta
-equality.
∎

Theorem I.2 (Theorem
7.2
).

With
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbb{N}}^{\omega}
as the parametrizing structure, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:\>\mathit{A}
in
GraD
, then
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{1}\mathit{A}
in LDC. Further, if
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
in
GraD
, then
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
in LDC.

Proof.

By induction on
GraD
typing judgment.
∎

Theorem I.3 (Theorem
7.3
).

With
\mathcal{L}
as the parametrizing structure, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
in
\text{DDC}^{\top}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}
in LDC. Further, if
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
in
\text{DDC}^{\top}
, then
\vdash\mathit{a}\leadsto\mathit{a^{\prime}}
in LDC.

Proof.

By induction on
\text{DDC}^{\top}
typing judgment.
∎

Appendix J
Derivations for Join and Fork in LDC(
\mathcal{L}
)

Proposition J.1.

\emptyset\vdash\mathit{c_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\to T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
where

\mathit{c_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:=\lambda\mathit{x}.\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{z}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{z})\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}),\text{ where }\eta_{\ell}\>\mathit{a}\triangleq(\mathit{a}^{\ell},\mathbf{unit})

.

Proof.

(1)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
[By rules
ST-VarD
and
ST-SubRD
]

(2)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A},\mathit{z}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{z}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}

(3)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{z}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{z}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
[From (
1
) and (
2
), by rule
ST-LetPairD
]

(4)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{z}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{z}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
[From (
3
), by rule
ST-SubRD
]

(5)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A},\mathit{y}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{y}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}

(6)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{z}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{z})\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
[From (
4
) and (
5
), by rule
ST-LetPairD
]

(7)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{z}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{z})\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}):^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}

∎

Proposition J.2.

\emptyset\vdash\mathit{c_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\to T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
where

\mathit{c_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:=\lambda\mathit{x}.\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}),\text{ where }\eta_{\ell}\>\mathit{a}\triangleq(\mathit{a}^{\ell},\mathbf{unit})

.

Proof.

(1)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
[By rules
ST-VarD
and
ST-SubRD
]

(2)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A},\mathit{y}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{y}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}

(3)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
[From (
1
) and (
2
), by rule
ST-LetPairD
]

(4)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}):^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}

(5)

\mathit{x}:^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\eta_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathbf{let}\>(\mathit{y}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\>\sqcup\>\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\_)\>\mathbf{be}\>\mathit{x}\>\mathbf{in}\>\mathit{y}):^{\bot}T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>T_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}

∎
